day.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Lately I wake up feeling like I’ve already done a full day’s work, and even simple things like standing at the register or cooking dinner for my family feel like they drain the last bit of energy I’ve got, no matter how much I try to rest on my days off.\n- S056 (Excessive_Anxiety_Worry): It feels like my brain is always running through worst‑case scenarios about money, the car breaking down, my mom getting sick, or my hours getting cut again, and even when nothing bad is actually happening I’m still tense like I’m waiting for something to go wrong.\n- S057 I’ve got nothing,’ and I can’t shut it off even when I tell myself it’s over the top and try to focus on something else.\n- S059 (Concentration_Difficulty): At work I’ll be in the middle of ringing someone up and suddenly realize I’ve zoned out, forgotten what I was doing, because my head drifted back to figuring out how we’re going to stretch the paycheck, and I have to shake myself to refocus on the customer.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Because my mind is always stuck on money and what could go wrong, my manager has talked to me twice about making My main issue is really just feeling constantly anxious and worried about everything, which leaves me completely worn out and exhausted all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in appetite or weight, or any feelings of hopelessness, guilt, or thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite and weight haven't changed, and I don't feel hopeless, guilty, or have any thoughts of hurting myself. It's really just the constant worry and this deep exhaustion that are wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, trembling, or feeling like something terrible is about to happen?"

In the past, I've generally been fine and never had any issues with my mood or energy like this before.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been feeling unusually worried or on edge most days, or having a lot of tension and anxiety along with these low-energy and low-mood symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel extremely worried and tense almost every day. My mind constantly runs through worst-case scenarios about money, the car, or my mom, and once it starts, it snowballs and I just can't shut it off. I constantly feel on edge, like I'm just waiting for the next thing to go wrong.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this worried and on edge most days—has it been happening for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been much longer than six months. It actually started about a year and three months ago when my store cut my hours and my work schedule became really unpredictable. Since then, this constant, worn-out worry has been hanging over me and just hasn't let up.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, have you also had trouble sleeping, muscle tension, restlessness, or feeling keyed up most days? These symptoms would help me tell whether this is more of an anxiety problem, a depression problem, or both.\n\nPatient: Yes, my sleep has been terrible—most nights I lie awake for hours with my mind racing, and if I do fall asleep, I wake up around 3 or 4 AM unable to drift back off. I also feel incredibly tense all the time, like I'm constantly waiting for something to go wrong. These sleep issues and tension have been going on for over a year now.\n\nDoctor: During the times you’ve felt down and lost interest, did those symptoms happen most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks, or are they mainly present when you’re worrying about different things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely tied to the constant worrying. My loss of interest in things like gardening and fishing happens because my mind is so exhausted from stressing about money and responsibilities all day. It's not really a separate feeling of being down, but more that the continuous mental strain just completely drains my energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, trembling, or feeling like something terrible is about to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had sudden panic attacks like that with a racing heart or shortness of breath. My anxiety is more of a constant, slow-burning worry and physical tension that just stays with me throughout the whole day.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you been feeling depressed or down most of the day nearly every day, even when you are not worrying about specific problems?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed or down in that way. My main issue is really just feeling constantly anxious and worried about everything, which leaves me completely worn out and exhausted all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in appetite or weight, or any feelings of hopelessness, guilt, or thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite and weight haven't changed, and I don't feel hopeless, guilty, or have any thoughts of hurting myself. It's really just the constant worry and this deep exhaustion that are wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, trembling, or feeling like something terrible is about to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those sudden, sharp spikes of panic or physical symptoms like a racing heart. It is much more of a constant, ongoing level of worry and tension that just stays with me throughout the day.\n\nDoctor: How much has this constant worry and tension affected your daily life, such as your work, home responsibilities, or relationships? Has it been hard to control the worry most days?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's incredibly hard to control because a single worry about rent quickly snowballs into fears of eviction, and I just can't shut it off. It has affected my work so much that my manager has talked to me twice about register mistakes, and at home, I'm too drained to do chores, go to church events, or spend time with my wife."

My anxiety is more of a constant, slow-burning worry and physical tension that just stays with me throughout the whole day.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you been feeling depressed or down most of the day nearly every day, even when you are not worrying about specific problems?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed or down in that way. My main issue is really just feeling constantly anxious and worried about everything, which leaves me completely worn out and exhausted all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in appetite or weight, or any feelings of hopelessness, guilt, or thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite and weight haven't changed, and I don't feel hopeless, guilty, or have any thoughts of hurting myself. It's really just the constant worry and this deep exhaustion that are wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, trembling, or feeling like something terrible is about to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those sudden, sharp spikes of panic or physical symptoms like a racing heart. It is much more of a constant, ongoing level of worry and tension that just stays with me throughout the day.\n\nDoctor: How much has this constant worry and tension affected your daily life, such as your work, home responsibilities, or relationships? Has it been hard to control the worry most days?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's incredibly hard to control because a single worry about rent quickly snowballs into fears of eviction, and I just can't shut it off. It has affected my work so much that my manager has talked to me twice about register mistakes, and at home, I'm too drained to do chores, go to church events, or spend time with my wife.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to control the worry most days, even when you know the concerns may be exaggerated or unlikely?\n\nPatient: Yes, I find it extremely difficult to control. A single thought about paying rent quickly snowballs into worst-case scenarios like getting evicted, my wife leaving, or not being able to help my mom. Even when I recognize these thoughts are over the top and try to force myself to focus on something else, I just can't seem to shut them off."

It is much more of a constant, ongoing level of worry and tension that just stays with me throughout the day.\n\nDoctor: How much has this constant worry and tension affected your daily life, such as your work, home responsibilities, or relationships? Has it been hard to control the worry most days?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's incredibly hard to control because a single worry about rent quickly snowballs into fears of eviction, and I just can't shut it off. It has affected my work so much that my manager has talked to me twice about register mistakes, and at home, I'm too drained to do chores, go to church events, or spend time with my wife.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to control the worry most days, even when you know the concerns may be exaggerated or unlikely?\n\nPatient: Yes, I find it extremely difficult to control. A single thought about paying rent quickly snowballs into worst-case scenarios like getting evicted, my wife leaving, or not being able to help my mom. Even when I recognize these thoughts are over the top and try to force myself to focus on something else, I just can't seem to shut them off.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, even when you are not worrying about specific problems?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel down, depressed, or hopeless like that. While I am extremely exhausted and overwhelmed, my main struggle is just this constant, tense worry about practical things like my bills and work shifts, rather than feeling hopeless."

It's really just the constant worry and this deep exhaustion that are wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any sudden episodes of intense fear or panic that peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest tightness, trembling, or feeling like something terrible is about to happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those sudden, sharp spikes of panic or physical symptoms like a racing heart. It is much more of a constant, ongoing level of worry and tension that just stays with me throughout the day.\n\nDoctor: How much has this constant worry and tension affected your daily life, such as your work, home responsibilities, or relationships? Even when I recognize these thoughts are over the top and try to force myself to focus on something else, I just can't seem to shut them off.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day, even when you are not worrying about specific problems?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel down, depressed, or hopeless like that. While I am extremely exhausted and overwhelmed, my main struggle is just this constant, tense worry about practical things like my bills and work shifts, rather than feeling hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries been present about a wide range of everyday things on most days for at least 6 months, and do you find them hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been worrying about a lot of everyday things like money, my car, my job, and my mom's health almost every single day for about 15 months now. It is incredibly hard to control, because once the thoughts start, they just snowball and I can't shut them off even when I try to focus on other things."
